In what field did a Swiss physicist who had a geometrical representation named after him work in?
A: ["Geometric class field theory", "List of things named after George Gabriel Stokes", "Bloch sphere", "We Had Him", "Sokol Did Not Love Him", "Cyril Sinelnikov"]
A: Geometric class field theory is an extension of class field theory to higher-dimensional geometrical objects: much the same way as class field theory describes the abelianization of the Galois group of a local or global field, geometric class field theory describes the abelianized fundamental group of higher dimensional schemes in terms of data related to algebraic cycles.
A: Exit? No.
A: ["Geometric class field theory", "List of things named after George Gabriel Stokes", "Bloch sphere", "We Had Him", "Sokol Did Not Love Him", "Cyril Sinelnikov", "Class field theory", "Non-abelian class field theory", "Timeline of class field theory", "Ring class field", "Conductor (class field theory)"]
A: Geometric class field theory is an extension of class field theory to higher-dimensional geometrical objects: much the same way as class field theory describes the abelianization of the Galois group of a local or global field, geometric class field theory describes the abelianized fundamental group of higher dimensional schemes in terms of data related to algebraic cycles. So the answer is: Geometric class field theory.
